In other countries too, relatively fewer research and development tasks are traditionally under attack in smaller businesses. ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e4316	Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00109/index.shtml -- 109/M]] What are the minimum fees for the Eurovignette and what special fees for the Alpine transit?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e4343	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e4359	Deputies! Let me first point out that the negotiations currently under way can only be considered useful if one sees the link between the debate on the increase of road charges in the framework of the Eurovignette or the Road Costs Directive and the bilateral negotiations between the European Union and Switzerland as regards cross-border transit. The objective pursued by Austria is not primarily to be paid, but rather to relieve, as far as possible, the sensitive Alpine region of transport flows of an environmentally harmful nature, i.e. to relocate to the railway and also to ensure that the shortest road is actually chosen and that it does not result from charges or other measures, as is the case with Switzerland's transport policy today. Our objective is, as far as tolls are concerned, to maintain, at least, the tolls in Austria today. There is now, as you are no doubt aware, a proposal from the European Commission aimed at protecting the two Alpine countries most affected by transit from Switzerland in such a way that the tolls actually required and taken by France in the Montblanc sector and Austria, particularly in the Brenner area, are preserved and provided as a general toll right for the Alps. To this end, the Commission's compromise proposal has been returned to include an appropriate Alpine clause in the EU Road Costs Directive. This proposal has received broad support from the last meeting of the EU Transport Council. This is our primary concern at the moment, because this is about the sensitive question of the Alpine transit and the relocation of the truck traffic in the order of 330 000 trucks per year to Switzerland. This is primarily not about the charge, but about the extent to which these negotiations will succeed to ensure that Switzerland opens a correspondingly large quota for 40-ton journeys through Switzerland at the first stage of the validity of the new agreement. Secondly, it is a question of whether Switzerland is quickly opening up a high-performance rail transport infrastructure for rail transit through Switzerland. As far as the values you have asked for are concerned, there is currently a proposal from the Dutch Presidency to increase road charges for inflation, from 1 250 ECU to 1 325 ECU at present. This is seen by many European countries, including Austria, as far from sufficient. On the other hand, there is Germany's call for an increase to at least 2 000 ECU, supported by Austria, and there is a compromise proposal from the Commission, which is aimed at raising the fees at least 1 650 ECU, which I have now reserved for details, because it is also intended to provide for differentiation in the level of pollution caused by the corresponding vehicle.

Dr. Graf: Double cashier!]] There have been over 600 opinions and opinions on this university study law according to the size of the reform work. In the future, only the respective study commission will be entrusted with the preparation of teaching and learning contents. That's what has already been said. In this way, decision-making and responsibility competence has been assigned to the universities, and it is up to the individual study committees to demonstrate innovativeness and to structure the range of studies better and clearer. With the present UniStG, the study commissions have already been given much responsibility and decision-making competence, as has been said. However, it has not been accepted for them to make the truly Australian decision whether Latin should be a prerequisite for their respective studies or not. In other words, in a number of studies such as languages, medicine or jus, Latin will continue to be a requirement for admission and the current university qualification regulation will continue to exist. However, apart from these last rudiments of late medieval traditions, the study commissions have been released into full might. The important thing is § 9, which leaves it to the respective study commissions whether they want to include the completion of a practice in the study plan or not. The definition of a practice has proved very successful in some fields of study, such as the Applied Business Administration in Klagenfurt, which has offered its own practical semester. And it is not the case, as Mr Graf said, that the semester of practice is missing, but it has been set up in general for the economics of the business sector eight semesters. It is thanks to the negotiators, Mr Niederwieser and Mr Lukesch, to say that they have succeeded in raising the hourly framework to 130 hours, and the business administration in Klagenfurt will continue to be able to offer the semester of practice within the framework of this new study system and one is very satisfied with this compromise. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: The practical semester will be deleted, you know!]] I'd like to tell you that. I spoke to the board of directors myself. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] A critical point may still be allowed: I am sorry that § 45 did not allow itself to be penetrated to abolish the old, unproven five-part scale of music. The first draft provided for the creation of a three-part scale, namely "excellent", "files" and "not existed". This would have adequately described the student's progress in studying rather than putting the student's achievements into an abstract number which, in its gradual classification from 2 to 3 or 3 to 4, is, in any case, completely irrelevant, because it does not decide anything. Nevertheless, one prefers to stay with the old five-part music scale, which is to be regretted. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: Now the teacher breaks through!]] Overall, however, there are a number of improvements for the students in the UniStG. The period for completion of the diploma thesis has been extended from three months to six months. In the future, the scope of the examination subjects must be designed in such a way that students can also cope with the study period in the normal course of study. The tests as an extraordinary listener or listener shall be counted unless they are used to circumvent repetition possibilities. In the future, certificates must be issued within four weeks after the examination has been made, which is important for study and family support, and the time-limits for recruitment have also fallen. It is also gratifying to charge courses, in particular the crediting of the pedak course for teaching and vice versa. However, the most serious changes are in the field of diploma studies – changes that have also been strongly criticized in some cases. For example, in the first draft in the field of mental and cultural studies, the elimination of the combination obligation and the reduction to six semesters were planned. A six-semester short study with academic degree, such as the Baccalaureate, which also leads to a formal qualification, would certainly have been a useful supplement in a newly-ordered, three-stage university sector. Unfortunately, one could not agree on such a procedure, so that the old study duration of eight semesters was left the same. However, the combination obligation has fallen final and the possibility of combination has been anchored, which is essentially concerned with the human and cultural studies, which have now been designed as individual studies. It was opened up by the free electorate a variety of new possibilities. In conclusion, I would like to say that this University of StG has taken a very important step towards reform which will strengthen university autonomy and thus also the responsibility for which the university section in the Federal Ministry of Science and Research has to express particular thanks. The Austrian People's Party as a coalition partner is part of the XVIII. The legislative period, if I remember correctly, is already signaling their approval, but it will then be withdrawn in the short term. That's the status quo. If passive voting rights are extended to all students from EU countries, this is undoubtedly a progress towards the existing legal situation, but one that I believe is too low. What if, for example, students from Switzerland or Liechtenstein are not passively entitled to vote? I'm just grabbing Liechtenstein out. You might say: after the few students! It doesn't matter. Liechtenstein is a small state and therefore does not have as many students as other countries, but Liechtenstein does not have its own university either. That is to say, the Liechtenstein students are forced to complete their studies either in Switzerland or in Austria – mainly in Innsbruck, colleague Lukesch. However, they do not come to the delight of passive voting rights. I don't understand. That is why I appeal again to the coalition partner not only to the Austrian High School Law quickly – for we can no longer afford to move it back to the next university elections – but also to innovation in a more comprehensive way.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e11237	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! First of all, I would like to deal with the amendment to the Telecommunications Act. I believe, in principle, that a great deal has already been done in this area, in the field of telecommunications as a whole. The postal monopoly has largely fallen, the terminals have been completely liberalized, we today have cable TV companies, energy supply companies, even the federal railways that are cracking on the cable monopoly, and the mobile phone monopoly has also fallen. That is, there is really something that happened, and we know that until the 1st century. Next year, the EU directives will also include a channelled voice-telephone monopoly. This liberalisation, which has already been implemented by us and is now being continued – as Mr Rosenstingl has said, it is too slow – is undoubtedly an opening to competition and has two objectives: on the one hand, the goal of attracting private capital and thus making these areas more efficient in the long term and facilitating European networking and cooperation, and on the other, the objective of increasing innovation, because more participants in the market will be trying to develop new product ideas, thereby reducing costs for consumers. I think that's an idea that carries the People's Party's manuscript. We have long called for this – the removal of monopoly, liberalisation – and we are in keeping with what the European Union is planning. Now let me comment on the question, which has taken so large space in the opposition, whether the monopoly is protected by this novelle. I can't see that. First of all, I see it as an intermediate step because we are not yet able to adopt the Telekom Act. However, the third frequency is already to be allocated, and this means, of course, more competition and no less competition. That is, by the intermediate step, the competition will come faster than if we waited until the telecoms law is finally passed. And the differing opinions show that it is still necessary to negotiate it. Secondly, it is clear that there is no point in talking about monopoly protection, because the two existing mobile networks, namely mobile communications and max. mobile, are not allowed to enter the 1800 frequency for three years. Now, as Mr Barmüller has done, we can say that there is an exception, only, Mr Barmüller, the faster the number of suppliers will be placed on the market, the more unlikely it is that one can fulfil these very strict conditions, under which only an independent opinion can be given to him, that he has no other option. This is really a very improbable variant, which was just made to take care of all events and not suddenly to have bottlenecks in the care. But in itself it is the result of the new who comes, first of all – in the construction – having a certain guarantee for his investment for three years; only a certain guarantee, because he has to find customers, and this, of course, also depends on his offer and his success. The second, however, is that there are two procedures. There is not only the administrative procedure, but also the auction procedure, and I would like to tell the Minister that my very personal view is that we would probably be well advised to take advantage of the auction procedure, because it clearly identifies the best bidder and does not depend on the many factors that competitions in the public are constantly being fought. I therefore think that the procedure we are making possible here is one that is in the interest of a corresponding liberalisation. Let me now say something about the second issue in this context, namely Austro Control. We certainly have very, very high air safety charges in Austria. This is a problem because, of course, it is not only our airlines, but also the airlines that land in Austria. But we want to remain an important flight cross. On the other hand, in this sensitive area, in which highly qualified people, namely the air traffic controllers, work under special stress and have a lot of responsibility – just as much responsibility, please, as the flight captains and copilots – for the safety of the flight, first-class services with which safety can be guaranteed over all the years, and – we have no strikes. We know what huge costs the airlines face when the air traffic controllers strike, because nothing goes. The ground staff at airports and the aircraft of the airlines will then be affected in the same way. The losses are huge! Foreign societies show us what balance sheet losses that means. This is something that an airline may also be able to ruin. This high quality of air traffic controllers, as well as their attitude to the profession, of course also requires a corresponding payment. The former Minister of Transport has now promised in 1994 on the occasion of the transformation into the new company, i.e. to the Austro Control, that, through collective agreement, the retirement of these air traffic controllers working under great stress should take place at 55 years. This corresponds to the international standard, which must also be added, because there is special stress in this area and therefore there are special safety requirements. It is certainly not possible for these amounts to be laid down from one day to the next, and therefore the solution has been found, analogous to the new foundations, that we can make a 20-year depreciation possible by this law. It is unfortunate that these burdens will, of course, lead to delays in reducing tariffs which are urgently necessary and likely and hopefully also intended. The objective must not be lost sight of – I would like to say that very clearly – but it will be delayed because these burdens must be added and picked up. The Committee's report says that there will not be any increases in fees, but, of course, we will not be able to have reductions in the next few days. You have to see that. Nevertheless, I think... [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Hofmann.]] Mr. Col., you're coming to me? [[Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Hofmann: There was up to 1 400 percent increase after dislocation!]] Look, you have to make an account, and I'll tell you again: I believe that what others have pre-examinationed to us on negative examples should be a warning. I know what I'm talking about. Look for Brussels, look for Spain, look for France! Take a look at what has been happening there with strikes from the air traffic controllers who have shut down everything that has paralysed half of Europe's air traffic. There is a lot at stake here, and a responsible overall transport policy must take this into account. I therefore believe that, from this point of view, we should be able and able to give our approval. The objective of further rationalisation, which also reduces fees in Austria, must not be lost sight of! I can add something from the knowledge of the European Union: We are invited to contribute to the fact that the 35 different systems of flight monitoring in Europe are combined into a single system, such as in America, because this would result in enormous savings and could significantly reduce losses and waiting times, because the waiting times in Europe are on average very, much longer than the average waiting times in the USA. And if we find partners within the framework of a public private partnership financing that are ready – and that is one of the goals – to take over the cost risk in the course of this construction, then this is the cheaper financing than if we do it ourselves. That is no question at all, and that is also the reason why we are going this way. However, if we do not find any offers in the search for an interest in which these interested parties take this risk, so only offers us things that we could just as well do on our own, then there is no reasonable reason to go this way. And that's what I've said in the last few days. In the interests of taxpayers – surprisingly, we agree on this – it is about financing sensible transport projects as economically and economically as cheaply as possible. We owe this to taxpayers – also from my side. [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] They're ninety-nine. In what precise form and to what extent do you expect private participation in the construction costs, and how much do you appreciate them? I can only answer this question after the private offers have been made. Tenths: What employment effect is expected By constructing this tunnel during construction itself and on the other hand by saving railways in operation afterwards? I answer this question as follows: General economic consequences and employment effects of public expenditure in the field of infrastructure have been demonstrated by many studies. I don't want to mention everyone in detail. The assessment of employment effects per billion public investment in these sectors ranges from 1,500 to 4 460 people per year and billion. The budgetary effects of unemployment, on the other hand, should be estimated, according to a Busch study, that a 1 per cent reduction in employment, which is roughly 29 000 to 30 000 additional unemployed, causes about 5 billion schilling direct and indirect costs to the state. The railway infrastructure investment programmes of around 200 billion Schilling on a price-based basis of 1995 will extend to 17 years – and to this period this 200 billion schilling – result in the following: an employment of about 26 000 employees over the duration of 17 years, that is, a total of about 440 000 years of employment, and a further return of money to the public hands of about 160 billion schilling, of which 120 billion are to the federal government. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] You ask in question 13 how much is the cost of running a tunnel through the tunnel, as you say, "extremely low" cost of construction costs if these construction costs are to be earned again, and whether it is right that an annual benefit fee of around 300 million schillings is planned, which would be about twice as much as before. Answer: Yes. I think that's realistic. Questions 16 and 18. They ask: "Did you think it was right to give up the south-east spange, especially at a time when spatial planners propose to include these projects in the range of projects to be co-financed by the EU, because of their Europe-wide importance?" "Are you aware that this route, in addition to the reduction of travel time between Vienna and Graz and Klagenfurt, could also lead to a much better development of the regions concerned, and through the Koralm Tunnel, would only create the prerequisite for a somewhat competitive offer in the rail transport of Graz – Klagenfurt, which can never be achieved by expanding the existing southern railway?" I answer this question as follows: The South East spange represents a long-term perspective for a capacity expansion in Vienna – Graz – Klagenfurt – the Italian state border, the so-called Pontebana axis, and is therefore not visible in the competitive relationship with the existing southern railway. Rather, in addition to the coral tunnel and the coral track, the southern railway is a powerful offer in this dimension. In addition, the South East Spange was included in the "Community Guidelines for Trans-European Networks", which should also maintain the importance of this high-performance link for the long-term expansion of the Central European Railway Network within the framework of the European Union. As a first step towards the realization of the most important sub-section of this long-term network supplement, which you, too, mentioned, was adopted on 29 July. In May 1995 between the Federal Government and the countries directly involved in Styria and Carinthia agreed that the necessary planning work for the Graz – Klagenfurt section, i.e. the Koralmbahn, should start immediately. Planning has been fully implemented. It should also be recalled, however, that the total transport span of which you are talking here has a time for implementation as an overall solution of 2020 and therefore cannot be put directly into relation with today's traffic requirements. ==References====External links== In October 1996 in Wolfsberg, the representatives of the countries of Styria and Carinthia stressed the importance of the Koralmbahn and expressed in a resolution which was transmitted to the Federal Chancellor. Aspects of regional development and development, especially in travel, clearly speak for the realization of a high-performance line in Vienna – Graz, in addition to the existing southern railway and the Semmering base tunnel, as well as the long-term forecasts for freight transport. The cost-benefit ratios were determined in the feasibility study according to both private and economic criteria, and for the Vienna – Graz section, the results were similar to those for the Graz – Tarvis section. It should be noted, however, that the section Vienna – Graz can only be used meaningfully when the Koralmbahn Graz – Klagenfurt is completed. At the same time, this means that the Vienna – Graz section will also have about 2020 as a time for final completion. But until then we will also need a more efficient southern railway especially in the interests of freight transport and in particular in the interest of protecting passengers on the roads from additional truck transport. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Your question 17 is: "Are you aware that this project will hardly be economically feasible if – as is specifically planned – a parallel route is built in Hungary and Slovenia and what consequences do you draw from it?" My answer is: I am aware that there is the intention of building a railway line between Slovenia and Hungary. However, the financing of this project has not yet been clarified in my knowledge. There is already a railway line from Slovenia via Croatia to Hungary relatively close to this projected line. So if cheap East price levels influence the route selection, Austria can already be bypassed today. The planned connection with Murska Sobota represents a reserve of external independence for Hungary and Slovenia. These reserves show – including other sectors, such as gas pipelines – that there is generally no one to finance them, because independence alone does not deserve any money. The project under consideration of a direct link between Slovenia and Hungary is being pursued within the framework of the pan-European Corridor V, in particular by Slovenia. According to the information currently available, the single-track restoration of a secondary railway is planned in a modern form between Hodos and Zalalövö, because only railway lines across Croatian territory are currently available in the course of Corridor V. As the subsequent rail network in Slovenia and Hungary is not electrified, the projected route is not currently being electrified. It is therefore assumed that this project will not have a decisive impact on the economics of the south-east spange. Question 19: "What considerations were important for the construction of the Galgenberg tunnel in the area of the 'Knot Obersteiermark', which will allow only a small reduction in travel time between Leoben and St. Michael, which is much more meaningful, because capacity-elevating Traidersberg tunnel, on the other hand, must continue to wait for realization as a connection to the Schoberpassbahn?" The answer is: The "Knot Obersteiermark" is a comprehensive solution to the problem of capacity between Bruck and St. Michael. The Galgenberg tunnel is only a first step in the development. Historically, the Leoben – St. Michael section was a subordinate link between two main railways, which today leads through a water protection area. In the context of networking planning, the need to establish the "Obersteiermark" node in connection with a future Koralmbahn is also being examined. However, a final outcome of these investigations is not yet available. Question 20: "Are you of the opinion that in recent years, in fact, the most important railway projects have always been started?" The answer can only be: Yeah, I'm that view. Question 21: "What are the main reasons for the fact that there is no system of priority in the field of transport infrastructure development to date, and when will such a system be implemented at last on the basis of the Federal Roadmap Plan or, as far as possible, in the preliminary form?" For the time being, a working group consisting of the companies Prognos, Basel, the Institute for Traffic Systems Planning of the Technical University of Vienna and the company IPE, Vienna, was commissioned to prepare a pilot study on the design and structure of such a federal road map. This pilot study was presented to the public in 1993. In line with the concept, the Federal Transport Road Plan will carry out a cross-carriage cost-benefit assessment. The concrete work on the Federal Transport Road Plan began in 1994. The work has been and will be carried out in constant coordination and coordination with other departments of the Federal Government, in particular with the Ministry of Economic Affairs, as well as in coordination with the federal states and the Austrian Federal Railways, as well as involving national and foreign experts. An essential prerequisite for such work is a high-quality data base. The question of ensuring mobility that is sustainable for people and the environment, as set out in the Convention on Labour, can only be answered if we have in depth knowledge of today's mobility and its impact. For example, a survey of 12 000 Austrian households was conducted to cover the daily transport needs of the Austrians. In addition, surveys have taken place with the aim of determining the Austrians' willingness to switch to more environmentally friendly transport when individual framework conditions, such as fuel prices, change. The results of the surveys are being evaluated. They can be found in comprehensive model calculations to predict the different scenarios of transport development and to illustrate the interactions between transport supply and demand. So the creation of the models is in full swing, as has already been said. A further preliminary work for the creation of this model calculation is the digital image of the Austrian transport networks. These works have not only been started, but have already been completed. These instruments will make it possible to change traffic demand in different transport policy strategies and to develop a well-coordinated approach to transport policies and development measures in the area of transport infrastructure. An appropriate assessment procedure, which allows for an assessment of the different strategies based on different criteria, is also in preparation. In line with the work agreement of the government parties of March 1996, which, in the context of the Federal Roadmap, states that – I quote – the preparation and gradual implementation of cross-road infrastructure concepts based on an agreed federal road map will be the focus of the future transport policy – the end of the quote – the completion of the Federal Transport Road Plan 1998 will take place. Due to the work done so far, the completion of the Federal Transport Road Plan has led to a master plan. This is now in the phase of completion. This Master Plan will already allow assessments of the future developments in transport networks, including possible alternatives in sub-areas, in order to assess on this basis the network compatibility of individual projects to be assessed in the priority order. The evaluation procedure for the project, especially in the first phase, will be as simple as possible and verifiable as possible in terms of the plausibility of the results. Question 22: "Are you ready to provide the National Council with the study on the priority of railway development projects on behalf of your home and the ÖBB infrastructure, if no, why not?" The study referred to above on the priority of railway infrastructure development projects has exclusively carried out a very closely defined business evaluation of the development projects and covers all economic and economic aspects. It is at the stage of the final discussion. However, I am of course willing to provide the relevant information at the request of the National Council. Question 23: "How high have the investments in individual rail infrastructure projects been in recent years, and what concrete financial success has been associated with this?" The planning and implementation of new and expanders in the Austrian rail network has been in progress since 1987. Since then, about 30 billion schilling have been implemented. This infrastructure investment was primarily used to create or maintain an international standard of the existing network. The increase rates, especially in Austrian rail freight transport, are not least due to improved railway infrastructure. In 1996, the volume of transport in rail freight transport could be increased to around 70 million net tonnes compared with 63 million net tonnes in 1990. Question 24: "What precise roadmap-based projects of the ÖBB are the basis of this infrastructure development, especially since the cycle roadmap NAT, for which a symmetrical clock node should be reached by means of the Semmering Tunnel, has been massively thinned in the Styria (direction West) and, for example, already deleted important daily border connections?" My venerations Ladies and gentlemen of the liberals! This question is also based on a lack of premise. The infrastructure project of the Semmering Tunnel is not based on any planned projects of the ÖBB at all. The meaning and purpose of the Semmering Tunnel – I have already pointed out – is certainly not in the possibility of symmetrical tactnotes in Wiener Neustadt and Mürzzuschlag by reaching a 30-minute edge travel time between these two stops. Such a tact node could at least be a gratifying side effect of the base tunnel. Rather, the construction of the Semmering Tunnel – it is to be repeated again – is intended to include a much stronger volume of goods in the future, the saving of traction energy, and the elimination of current very unfavourable technical parameters of the Semmering belt line. There is no link between recent changes in the supply of public transport services by public transport and the construction of the Semmering Tunnel, since the construction of long-term transport infrastructures cannot be made dependent on the annual timetable for the public transport of public transport by public transport. Short debate on the reply to the question 1770/AB
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e14140	We are now entering the brief debate on the question answered by the Federal Minister for Finance with the order number 1770/AB. The answer to this question has been distributed, and a reading by the author is therefore insufficient. We are going into the debate. I would point out that according to § 57a para. 1 of the Rules of Procedure no speaker may speak for more than 5 minutes, but the first speaker has a speaking time of 10 minutes for reasons. The opinions of the members of the Federal Government or of State Secretarys should not last more than 10 minutes. Mr Oellinger, you are the applicant, the signatory of the request. I would ask you to open the debate.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e14174	Mr President. Mr. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Unfortunately, in the copy of the written answer to your question, the introductory text lacks the justification for the request. But I'm going to be happy to submit them orally. In principle, when asked to the Federal Minister, we were asked whether foreign citizens, primary foreign citizens who work here in Austria and who have lost family support through the savings package number 2 had to be determined because their children are constantly residing abroad – that is the wording in § 5 para. 4 Family tax compensation law is laid down – not actually according to the law, according to the Income Tax Act, the maintenance deduction amount is due. It is clear that the removal of the family allowance also removes the amount of child replacement for those children who are permanently residing abroad. I would like to stress the word "permanent" because it does not have a minor meaning in the justification of the question answered by the Federal Minister. The Income Tax Act sees in § 33 para. 3 lit. (b) a different wording. There it says: A taxable person – and the wording is unfortunately very complicated – for a child who does not belong to his household (section 2 para. 5 Family compensation law 1967) – which defines what a household is – and for which neither he nor his non-permanent spouse is granted family allowance, which provides legal support, a maintenance allowance of 350 S per month is permitted. – That increases with the number of children. It is clear from this provision of the Income Tax Act that under certain conditions – the child does not live in the household – the maintenance allowance is granted. It is then referred to § 2 para. 5 of the Family Allowance Act, where it is defined what a household is. This justification is of course important: A child belongs to the household of a person if it shares an apartment with that person in the event of uniform economic management. The membership of the budget is not deemed to be withdrawn – and this is especially the case. a interesting – if the child only temporarily stops outside the common apartment. - It's just temporary. If the child is only temporarily staying outside the common home, it is nevertheless a common household. We have previously heard – so the corresponding provision – the maintenance withdrawal amount according to § 33 is only allowed if no common budget exists. And that's the point I want to make. Minister! It is totally incomprehensible why You answer this question in a way that you say these children are considered to be members of the household. If one reads through these provisions of the Family Allowance Act, including the explanations and court rulings, then it is clear that even if these children are only temporarily defined in the explanations, what is "temporary" - belongs to a household, the family allowance is granted. On the other hand – and this is now the point – is argued in the family tax compensation law, children of foreign nationals – although it does not stand in it because it is neutrally formulated – who stay permanently abroad, this family allowance is not permitted. Now, in this explanation of the answer, the children are only temporarily staying abroad. If this is your reason for refusing the maintenance allowance, then these children currently abroad because they are not permanently abroad are entitled to the family allowance. But now you have removed the family allowance from the family allowance law in the context of the savings package 2 because the children stay abroad constantly. And now everything is imaginable, Mr Minister, but only one thing: that the child is constantly staying abroad according to the one target paragraph, but that, according to the other objective, the same child does not reside permanently abroad, it is only temporarily separated from his household, i.e. a household member. Minister! This answer is unacceptable. Either – and indeed this law is cleverly also – an adult taxpayer for the child he has to provide is entitled to the family allowance and the amount of the child's allowance because he is just carrying the family burden: To provide for children, or if he does not receive this family allowance and the amount of child replacement for certain reasons, but is nevertheless obliged to care for the child, he is at least entitled to the maintenance allowance. This reply, Minister, is – I can't say otherwise – the cynical paradox. It is therefore cynical because, on the one hand, the same person is defined as a person who is permanently resident abroad, and in the Income Tax Act, your side, not the law itself, defines and determines, it does not stay permanently abroad. So that's not possible! It is cynical because of the fact that the same person from both titles, family support, family compensation and family support, who is denied support and from the other alternative title, which was, of course, designed in such a way as to apply to this case, is also denied the support of a taxable person for his tax burden from family support. This, Minister, is not possible! This is unacceptable, Minister. And something else: The reason you gave in the answer to the question is also cynical. That is the second paradox, because you say that this does not mean discrimination against the maintenance of children living abroad, because the home country of the child, in the form of cash or in-service benefits, for example, training opportunities, health care, a corresponding charge for children. This is also cynical, Mr Minister, because you and I, both of us, and all here know that the tax burden this person carries is in Austria. The person working here in Austria finances the Austrian social and tax system. The person who works here in Austria carries their burdens for the society here. It therefore also has a right to appropriate support here. Not Turkey is responsible for caring for the children of these people. ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e14914	Mr President. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen! A few words and thoughts on the novel of the Austro-Control-Law. The Austro Control Ges.m.b.H., responsible for the safe and economic flow of air traffic, must ensure that this safety is ensured, from the occasion of the engines to the departure of the Austrian airspace and vice versa. There are up to 2,500 controlled aircraft daily in or over Austria. The Austro Control employs 1,000 employees, of which 300 are fully trained pilots. The Austro Control was formed in 1994 by the Federal Office for Civil Aviation, founded in 1955, is owned by the Republic, but is privately organized. Their legal basis was the Austro Control Ges.m.b.H. In 1993, it was created. What tasks does she have to perform? – Air traffic services, control services, information and alert services, compliance monitoring, air traffic information service, air traffic reporting service, air traffic weather service, air traffic control, air traffic control, approval of air traffic controllers, approval of air traffic control equipment, approval of air traffic control equipment, air traffic control, search and rescue service, establishment and operation of air traffic control facilities, issue of civil aviation personnel certificates with respect to and supervision of civil aviation schools. That is, there is a wealth of responsibilities and tasks that this Austro Control Ges.m.b.H. As announced, it may also be used for other airspaces. It may be interesting to note that in the mid-1980s a real explosion of air traffic took place: If there were still 337 986 movements in 1987, they rose in 1996 to 685 053, i.e. to more than double. The instrument flights are included here. Overflights rose by 100 000 between 1993 and 1996, with arrivals and departures by around 40 000 over the same period. Aviation – which is explicitly stated – is the only mode of transport that carries its own infrastructure costs and, as required by the EU as an objective, also practices cost-truth. ==References== The collective agreement entered into force in January 1996 for the staff of the Austro Control Ges.m.b.H., which is available to us, fulfils the commitment made to the exclusion that a transitional arrangement for air traffic controllers from 55 onwards. ==References====External links== This is in line with the international standard of early retirement for long-term air carriers. The amendment allows the society to write down the provisions to be made for this transitional scheme – spread over a period of 20 years. The establishment of provisions for the personnel costs of air traffic control would have been possible even before the division of the Federal Office for Civil Aviation by including these costs into the cost base of air traffic charges, but was prevented by the classification of the federal budget. Therefore, the Austro control needs to be remedied in this regard. The advantage here is: There is no cost to the Federation, there is full EU conformity, and there is a committee established that the Committee on Transport assumes that the additional burden resulting from the provision of staff provisions for personnel will not lead to an increase in air navigation charges. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e15241	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! From air to water: I would like to make a few comments about the Shipping Act. ==References== In January 1990, several EU regulations entered into force, it was more than sensible to present a new draft of the Shipping Act. This is the case today. I very much hope that this draft law will receive the broad approval of this House, primarily because we have already been given certain positions from the FPÖ, by Mr Grollitsch, in the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, although we are partly divided. Ladies and gentlemen! It's about rafting. We have succeeded in defining rafting by law, and we also allow national leaders to impose restrictions on certain shores of wild waters by means of their own shipping police regulations. In my view, this is a very essential instrument. It concerns mainly those tourist-oriented states in which rafting plays a role, such as Salzburg, Tyrol and Styria. This is where the heads of state can now intervene meaningfully. We must also remember that there is a patent requirement for the leaders of private rafts and that a one-month driving practice on wild waters is laid down by law as a condition for the approval for examination. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that this is the crucial point: that, as far as the ship's leaders are concerned, we do not rely on training, but on the practice, and we set this priority. A ship's guide, to which a large number of people are entrusted, must first and foremost know on which waters it is moving and which risks there are given. This cannot completely compensate for theoretical training. It is true that rafting is basically a risk sport and is equipped with a high risk potential. Nobody can rule out accidents, but it is a matter of increasing the range of offers – and I could imagine very well that Mr Peter agrees with me. In the tourism sector, the range of offers should be as wide as possible. If there are tourists who want to make this form of adventure belief, then we should offer them the opportunity, but in a secure legal situation and in particular with the limitation that the protection of life must be guaranteed to a very high degree. That is why I can invite you, ladies and gentlemen, to give your consent to this shipping law, especially because we have not only implemented important EU directives, but have taken a very reasonable step towards the future.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e15473	Mr President. Federal Minister! In my contribution to the debate, I would like to deal with the shipping law and petition number 13. [[President Dr. Brauneder shall take over the chairmanship.]] The Shipping Act came into force in 1990 and, as a result of the experience gained since then, various adjustments are necessary. In particular, it is necessary to make additional changes in terms of competence, the implementation of EU directives and the implementation of international rules. Mr Grollitsch referred to rafting. I would like to point out some of the shipping where adaptations to the international ship agreement are taking place. In the interests of administrative relief and simplification, facilitation of the approval of ship managers' schools shall be standardised and the procedure shall be simplified. Ladies and gentlemen! For reasons of clarity and legibility, the entire "Schiffahrtsgesetz 1990" as "Schiffahrtsgesetz" will be adopted again. Formal is a new law. The federal law gives rise to considerable savings or additional revenue for the covenant; that is the delight of this law. Some examples: The authorisation for use for sports equipment is missing. The recurring review of ferry facilities is no longer carried out annually, but in the future in the three-year period. In addition, the official equinox is absent. There is no obligation on vehicles on non-water roads or on passenger ships. The first review of CE-marked sports vehicles and the reciprocity check on foreign qualifications are also generally absent. A key point: The review of the physical and mental suitability of owners of captain's patents no longer takes place every seven years, but is in the future only starting from 65. Year of life required. The problem of alcohol on the steering wheel was discussed in the Committee. This is regulated in § 6. There it says: To guide a vehicle, as in road transport, the 0.8-promille alcohol limit must not be exceeded. The amendment tabled by the Committee has reduced the limit of alcohol to 0.1 per mille for commercial shipping. Ladies and gentlemen! This law has made a number of improvements. In particular, red tape has been taken into account. I would like to vote in favour of this bill. Now to the petition "20 years of flight noise are enough – the Danube city demands its right". This petition was granted to the Transport Committee and on 21 December. ==References== ==References====External links== The petitioners request the National Council to combat the increasing noise pollution in the Danube city. As early as the construction of the 16 slopes, there were difficulties, which was largely rejected from the beginning. Some 40,000 signatures have been collected. The population is particularly burdened by the apparent excess of the load limit of up to 17 percent of total air traffic, which is said to have been promised by the competent ministers at the time. Similarly, the promise not to start towards Donaustadt is increasingly broken. The binding commitment of all political groups to the introduction of a night flight ban has not been fulfilled. Furthermore, the required minimum height on approach has not been reached. In relation to the total number of air traffic in scheduled and chartered traffic last year, 15.3 percent of landings via Eßling took place last year from January to October. This means a 1 per cent reduction compared to the previous year. Starts towards Donaustadt will only be carried out by the west wind if the second runway is blocked due to construction work or if the longer slopes are needed for long-haul flights for safety reasons. With regard to the required night flight ban during the period from 10 a.m. to 6 p.m., it is found that there is no absolute ban on night flights at any airport in Europe, as requested in petition 13. In order to minimise noise pollution during the night hours, it was established in the 1993 Civil Aviation Regulation that the Vienna airport is only used during the night hours by the so-called Chapter III aircraft, which is currently the quietest category of jet aircraft. The EU Directive 92/14 provides for a ban on the nearest category of aircraft, the so-called Chapter II aircraft, per 1. ==References== Then further reduction of the maximum levels caused by the flight noise is expected. An absolute ban on night flights for Vienna Airport would have a negative impact on economic efficiency – for example, long-distance flights in the Far East would not be feasible to the present extent – and on the other hand, as in comparable European airports, there would be many derogations and therefore no significant reduction in flight noise. According to a survey carried out recently by the Austrian Central Office, two thirds of the population in Vienna feel disturbed by noise caused by road transport at night, but only 0.8 percent by air noise. In order to justify non-compliance with the minimum altitude when flying: All instrument approach to the airport shall be checked by means of radar as regards the required flight altitude and course. These data are recorded in full and monitored constantly. The non-compliance with the minimum flight altitude also makes no sense, for no pilot willfully suspends the lives of his passengers, his crew and his own life to a safety risk. At the Transport Committee meeting of 7. A motion for a resolution by Mr Parnigoni and Mr Kukacka was adopted by a large majority on March: The Federal Minister for Science and Transport is invited to continue initiatives to further improve the air noise situation in the vicinity of Vienna-Swechat Airport. I'll get you together. We should try to keep an eye on the overall outlook in this area. On the one hand, I understand the concerns of the people concerned, on the other hand, we also recognise the efforts of the responsible persons – the airport Vienna AG – to take measures to reduce the loads on the affected neighbouring people on a regular basis.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e15737	Ladies and gentlemen! I too would like to make a few comments on the Shipping Act. It is a relatively young law. It only entered into force in 1990. More recently, it is mainly about allowing some EU directives and EU regulations to be incorporated into our law. In essence, there are three new EU directives. Firstly, the Sports Boat Directive, which concerns the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to sport boats. The second point concerns the Shipper Directive, which is the harmonisation of the conditions for the acquisition of individual state-owned patents. Thirdly, it is a draft ECE Convention on the International Transport of Dangerous Goods on Internal Roads. Further EU directives will be reinforced in this novel, on the one hand, the Patent Recognition Directive and on the other hand the Authorisation Recognition Directive. Some of the changes also concern those areas which are now taking place as a result of the experience gained since the entry into force. In essence, it is ten priorities to read in the government template and in the amended law. It is mainly about simplification. ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e15794	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! You're supposed to take petitions seriously. But the air traffic is partly perceived as particularly disturbing, especially in the evening and night hours, and that is why it is a subject to be discussed. Mr Edler has signed a petition, the Citizens' Initiative No. 13, which also came into the high house, and there are six points in it. The six points have to be analysed, namely whether the claims are justified first, if they are correct and thirdly feasible. We have always made the same mistakes in Austria. We have built airports without keeping the necessary spatial planning regulations – I think of the Salzburg airport, where until ten years ago construction grounds were identified in the Sound Limit of the airport and new buildings were created there. Of course, people there bought reasons for little money to say now: The planes are bothering us! Humanly, understand everything. However, we have already had to experience at Salzburg airport, due to the lack of regional planning policy, that the possible utilization of the airport has been significantly reduced. If one had previously responded correctly to the spatial order, that would not have been necessary. In a similar situation, we are now at Vienna Airport. I would just like to point out the following before we talk about the individual points: All the restrictions we have unilaterally imposed in Austria – unilaterally, as we say – especially in the area of Chapter II aircraft, which today are mostly or almost more flown by our eastern neighbours, who are not in the European Union, with whom there are bilateral treaties, are based on the reciprocity principle that there will be retortion measures for our own airline. If you ban Chapter II aircraft in Austria, this means that the Bela-Russia-Air of Belarus will say: The flight from the AUA to Minsk will be deleted because we only have Chapter II aircraft, and if we are not allowed to land with them in Vienna, then of course – reziprok – the Vienna flight – Minsk will be deleted. This would hit our own airline, the AUA, very hard. I would point out that each thing has two sides and therefore it is worth looking at things more precisely in detail and not just saying: Petitions – Vote, I am already in favour of it. This is the populism that is most often accused of the liberals in this house. The first point is therefore a 17 per cent load limit of the flight volume. Amazingly, this 17 percent border was never reached, but a maximum of 16.5 percent of landings in 1996 were on this track. So the 17 percent are not to be mounted because they have never been reached. The second point is the introduction of a "Beautiful Weather ILS". This is – simply said for the laymen – that you do not make the approach or landing straight, but turn a curve immediately after the start. The noise pollution at takeoff is much greater than the noise pollution in landing. The noise load at landing comes from the weight of the machine. The harder a machine is, regardless of which engines it has, which run only at a very low speed when landing, the louder it is. That is, the noise is caused by the weight of the machine and not by the speed. So it's obviously about the start. However, according to the international guidelines of the International Civil Aviation Organisation, it is not possible to order this to a maximum of 5 per cent deviation from the approach base. This is a call for non-fulfilment of all international rules. Third point: immediate introduction of a night flight ban in the period from 10 a.m. to 6 p.m. Today, a request has been made by Dr Graf, similar to the Munich example. In Munich the night flight ban applies from 23:00 to 5:00. In the time before 23:00 and after 5:00 there are more flights in Munich than there are in Vienna overall at night. In Vienna there are on average 18 landings and 9 take-offs during the night, and of which only 17 percent are on this route. I therefore believe that we are talking about an order of magnitude which I consider to be tolerable. What I can imagine – that was the proposal of Barmüller and Wabl – is that there is a night-start and landing ban for Chapter II aircraft – that is also compatible with the reciprocity, because we do not start there at night and do not land and have no need for it at all. From 2002, only more Chapter III aircraft – these are the silent aircraft – will be allowed in the European Union, and the Chapter II aircraft will slowly disappear from the market due to their age. But, of course, our Central and South-East European partners will have to continue flying with old devices from purely financial means. I think that this is a good idea for Barmüller and Wabl to rule out at night. One should enter into negotiations with the airport in this respect, not according to Munich's model, but according to its own rules of play. We do not need the Munichers to explain to us how it is. They say: Chapter III aircraft shall be subject to a reduction in the frequency or a clearly defined number of frequencies. I would point out, of course, that every total ban on night flights has an effect on the Austrians themselves, because the approval of charter flights is therefore possible because the liner machines used on the day are on the way in the charter at night or at the edge of the hours and therefore at much cheaper costs Austrians fly on holiday than would otherwise be possible. It hurts me as a hoteler. I can agree with you immediately on a kerosene tax, but it will have to be introduced worldwide or at least throughout Europe. - But that's another subject. Fourth point: complete control of the required approach procedures – minimum flight altitude. - That's an outrageous nonsense! No pilot in the world will not respect the minimum flight altitude and thus endanger itself and its passengers. This statement is absolutely rebuttal. There are precise controls of flight rules. There is the so-called FANOMOS system – Flight Track and Noise Monitoring System – which records perfectly where someone is flying. This fourth point, Mr Edler, which you signed – you have not asked enough – is not true. The minimum flight altitude shall not be lowered. It's wrong to say that. The question of noise pollution, I said first, is something subjective. The noise pollution is below all limits. The limit values are once again lower at night by 10 decibels because they are perceived as even more disturbing during the night. However, I believe that this question should be approached by a gradual reduction in night air traffic and a total ban on Chapter II aircraft at night from 10 a.m. to 6 p.m. This would be the constructive way to get out of the current situation. An immediate ban on Chapter III aircraft is, of course, a fool, because these are the modern ones. They mean the Chapter II aircraft. That's a write mistake, I'm correcting him with this. – This is not correct and unacceptable for the reasons I have mentioned. I therefore believe that the petition should have the meaning that the High House and, above all, the Ministry of Transport – the Minister is sitting here – really devoting this question and, together with the Austrian Airlines, together with the Vienna Airport, finding a solution that reduces noise, if it is just a subjective – it is objectively below the authorised limits – for the population. But let us do this together with the airport and with the AUA and not with Ho-ruck requests to be sold at the next election meeting. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e15871	Mr Sigl is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e15893	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! In his speech, Mr Barmüller said that he could imagine a five-party request for the reduction of the promille limit to 0.5. The Socialists would certainly like to agree with this request if the signature of the honourable Member Mag. Kukacka would be found. Then the obstacle of the five-party proposal would have been overcome. [[Abg. May. Barmüller: He never does!]] [[Abg. May. Kukacka: I know that one third of the SPÖ delegates are against it too! First, take care of your own group!]] In the Shipping Act, we have also created the possibility to lower the Promille limit to 0.1. So I see very positive in the future that new ways can be taken here. Ladies and gentlemen! I spoke to address a number of points of discussion on the Trans-European Railway Agreement. Since 1995, Austria has participated in the trans-European railway project, in short TER, with the signing of the Trans-European Railway Trust Fund. The objectives of this project include the improvement of railway infrastructure in the project states, the development of investment and financing programmes for individual north-south connections for the purpose of increasing capacity, improving railway technology and technology as well as the organisational and management structure of railway undertakings. The financing of the management and organisation necessary for the implementation of the TER project shall be ensured by the Trans-European Railway Lodging Fund, whose members make an annual contribution to this Fund. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! TER is not only the only international organisation dealing with these objectives, but also in line with the principles of Austrian transport policy. For example, the Trans-European Railway Agreement, as well as the Austrian transport policy for the shift from road to rail, is entering into force. In particular, with regard to the opening of the east, participation in the TER appears to be an appropriate means of combating the threat of goods transiting from the East. Because of the increasing economic interrelationships between the regions in Europe, freight traffic has increased above average. Unfortunately, these increases in freight transport are mainly dealt with through road freight transport, thereby multiplying the already high external costs of road freight transport, such as the cost of accidents or environmental costs. In order to counter this trend, the TER Member States are trying to develop transport infrastructures that drive the use of environmentally friendly modes of transport. And since the railway is probably the most efficient and at the same time most environmentally friendly transport institution, it is only possible to support the development of a coherent and efficient rail and combined transport network in the countries of Central and Eastern Europe, as provided for in the TER programme, because only efficient rail and a functioning combined transport network guarantee a lasting problem of freight transport in Europe. Ladies and gentlemen! A modern transport infrastructure not only ensures better quality of life for the population and the protection of the environment, but also ensures trade at the same time. In this respect, I also think the financial contribution that Austria would have to make for the years 1997 to 2000 in the amount of around 450 000 S, if we want to continue to belong to this association, as quite good investment for the future. According to the budget planning, this contribution is used for personnel costs, training, technical material procurement and much more. As the last reason for further membership of the TER, I would just like to say that Austria is the only EU country to belong to the TER, which guarantees Austria a special status within the TER. Furthermore, the objectives of the TER are EU-compatible, as the EU's trans-European networks are based on the TER corridors and the EU explicitly refers to the TER in various declarations. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! In conclusion, I would like to say that we Social Democrats are in favour of continuing membership of the Trans-European Railway Trust Fund, that Austria's active participation in the TER is not only in our interest but also in the interests of Europe. Thank you.

In such a situation, in which one knows, there will be something like that in the next few years, there will be an EU uniform system, but one does not yet know which – one cannot anticipate it because it is not yet known – it is most likely to remain in the existing system, develop it and enter new land only when it is specified by the EU. We have chosen this way with this law. Next point: There was a great debate as to whether objectivity and diversity of opinion in the individual institutions or external plurality, i.e. diversity through various institutions in an area or also through various media in an area – newspapers, posters, broadcasting – ie the effort to provide comprehensive information on a radio or in an area through various radios or other media. In the draft, we have been aware of this – and we have remained in the process of expressing objectivity and diversity in individual radio. I know that some believe that the model of external plurality is the fairer, the more comprehensive. This may be true in areas where there is unhindered access to a medium, i.e. where everyone can bring out a newspaper and therefore commensurate with this diversity. However, in an area where there is only limited access due to a certain number of frequencies, this outside, i.e. this total plurality cannot be guaranteed at all, in an area where only a radio is possible, because there is only one frequency in this valley. In view of this situation, it is therefore more reasonable to stay in the case of objectivity and diversity of opinion as a task for the individual operator, as this ensures the whole. The minorities have already been mentioned. I think there's been a good way to go. We will thus enable connected radios in the languages of minorities and thus also fulfil international obligations. Point First reading of motion 375/A by Dr Hans Peter Haselsteinerand comrades concerning a federal law amending the Stock Exchange Act 1989
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e18265	This leads us to the 16th place. Agenda item. President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Helmut Peter. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-03-20-020-XX-NRSITZ-00067_d7e18293	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! I must ask you to lend me your ear in an important matter in spite of an hour later. The Austrian Stock Exchange is referred to as "VIP" by many foreign observers, which is the acronym for "Vienna Insider Place". That's unfortunate, and that's not good. The Austrian stock exchange has a "bean culture" that I can compare with that in Albania if I want to be evil. But we all agree that we must do something. We are moving towards investor protection and minority protection, because we believe that this is the most important point also in all the other reforms that the Federal Government is already planning to take priority. We are pleased that this Federal Government climate Mr Ruttenstorfer, State Secretary of the Ministry of Finance, has commissioned the Ministry of Finance to deal with the reform of the stock exchange in Austria in its own field of activity. We regret, however, that no visible and noticeable activities are evident. We have already pointed out, on the occasion of the turbulent privatisation and the sale of the Creditanstalt-Bankverein to Bank Austria, that the minority shareholders of the credit institution of this acquisition are virtually unprotected. We also asked a question to the then Minister of Finance climate, which at that time answered us very rudimentary. We hope that he will now be more devoted to this task as Federal Chancellor. In addition, we have tabled the motion for a resolution concerning the 1989 Stock Exchange Act in the House, which is being submitted today to the first reading. This is about the mandatory offer with which the acquisition of one company by another, when a certain proportion of the voting rights – we have accepted 33 1/3 percent of the existing and purchased voting rights – is exceeded, following the international practices to be reported by the acquirer in good time of the stock exchange supervision and the company whose shares he takes over. Secondly, he has a similar offer to make to minority shareholders. That's an international custom. Why not in Austria? We discussed research at the beginning of this two-day Parliamentary meeting, and I applied that, although research funding is important, one will not be able to finance research, produce product maturity and finance market penetration with the product if the operation is not provided with the necessary risk capital. In Austria, a large number of funds are in private hands, namely, 3 800 billion schilling! However, the value of stock exchange capitalisation is only 4 percent of Austrian stockholders and only 16 percent of Austrian gross domestic product. In view of this, I say once again the evil sentence, although it hurts me to have to say it: Austria is really the last stock exchange location in Albania! This is a real disaster for our country! I therefore call on the Federal Government and the High House, especially the government parties, to turn to this question at last. It does not make sense to comment on the forthcoming framework directive of the European Union, which could perhaps become a mere Council recommendation by resisting Britain. Acting is a real necessity in this country, so that a stock exchange culture can be developed to ensure that risk capital also flows into companies that then implement new innovations and thus continue in employment. [[Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Schöggl: What is an "old innovation"?]] I know this was a pleonasm now! But I am glad that you are so careful and correcting me! I'll focus again late at night! Thank you very much! The change in the right of acquisition is a condition for a Europe-wide share market culture, which must not be limited to the domestic market. The Austrian stock exchange should also be interesting for foreign investors! This is an essential part of an innovative capital market policy which is long overdue in the light of the start of the common economic and monetary market. There are bitter forecasts by economic researchers who believe that the Vienna Stock Exchange has already fallen asleep in the possibility of becoming a centre of competence for the capital markets of Eastern and Central Europe. The last chance to prevent this falling asleep and still jump on the moving train is the realization of economic and monetary union. She's about to go. We therefore need a competitive, competitive stock exchange of European standards. Ladies and gentlemen! I ask you, above all the ladies and gentlemen of the coalition parties, but also my colleagues from the opposition groups, to really address this issue with the necessary urgency. We do not have the time to continue to afford a sleepy stock exchange at the Schottenring. We need a new stock market culture, we need a new stock exchange law, we also need to create a stock exchange for small and medium-sized enterprises, and we should also go in the direction of small companies. I would ask you once again to address this important question. - Thank you.

For this reason, it is justified that the minority shareholder will receive less than the one who buys the entire package. But that this discount is covered by 25 per cent, I think it is necessary to protect institutional small investors, for example, from abrupt losses in value. In addition, this also protects the minority shareholder, because in the strategic sense of saying that the package would have been worth 50 percent more, I do not think it justified. – However, these 25 percent would accept the ÖVP. In this case, however, we must standardize an exception to the mandatory minority right for the family-ags, especially in succession. And it is also necessary to provide for an exception to the specific reorganisation case, because if someone is already ready to reorganise a totally marode society, then it is not to prevent a high protection price for minority shareholders from rehabilitating it. The EU Commission has been informed that Eliash has given contractual assurances to make its own contribution of 300 million schillings within three years. That's absolutely wrong! Eliasch has never accepted this commitment, so the entire banking consortium is now extolling itself. Not only does Eliasch not want to repay the 900 million schilling that he has so far been abandoned, but he also wants to make further losses of 800 million schilling so that he does not have to make equity grants that he has to make. Nevertheless, the Austrian authorities, including this government and its members, have informed the EU Commission that this is true with these 300 million schillings. This is a conscious misinformation. The EU Commission has been informed that Austria had awarded tobacco a contract for the privatisation of HTM. – There has never been such a one! Instead, a certain Mr. Treichl, the son of the former Director-General of the CA Treichl, was working on behalf of a foreign bank to try to privatise Austria's tobacco and was then used by Austria as a sanator for HTM. Although this Mr Treichl was still working in early August for the renovation of this company and at least 500 000 S of hand money for his monthly advisory work, he suddenly held 14 days later that this company had to be sold because it was no longer remedial. There's everything together: Minister of Finance Staribacher forces the management of Austria tobacco to resign, puts it down, employs interim directors in the person of his own secretaries, who at the same time completes liability insurance because they say: We are not at all suitable to carry out the renovation, and if we have something to do with it, then the liability insurance must pay for us the damage. Then the EU Commission is misinformed, Eliash does not meet his return obligations and the Austrian taxpayers lose 3.6 billion schilling. Then, in addition to the 900 million schilling, the Austrian banks have to drop another 800 million schilling, and then 600 jobs are lost. Three Finance Ministers played with: Lacina, Staribacher and climate! That is why we believe that in the case of a debacle of 3.6 billion schilling, while losing hundreds of jobs, and threatening that this company will be in bankruptcy at all, which in the end has been nothing but spree, it would be necessary to look for responsibility for those who have misinformed the EU Commission. For the EU Commission would not have agreed that Austria should give the HTM the high level of funding, because the owner is willing to raise capital, this debacle would not have existed!
